points in the flow at a given instant. The paths of the fluid particles in turbulent flow are extremely complicated, resulting in an extensive mixing of the
fluid.

To try and formulate such a random field–and inspired by
Propositions(2.7)
and
(2.8)
– we make the following general propositions. We take a smooth flow which at least a weak solution of the NS equations and nonlinearly ’mix’ this flow with a classical random field, specifically a BF random field to create a new random field or random turbulent flow, utilising the averaged Reynolds number as a ’control parameter’ to adjust the degree or magnitude of randomness or turbulence.

Proposition 2.26
.

(
Construction of a random vector field representing a turbulent flow
)
Let
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\bm{\mathrm{R}}^{3}
be a domain with volume
Vol[\bm{\mathfrak{D}}]\sim L^{3}
and
let
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
contain an incompressible fluid of velocity
U_{a}({x},t)
that is smooth or laminar for all
(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\times[0,\infty)
so that
\nabla_{a}U^{a}=0
. The fluid has viscosity
\nu
. Here,
U_{a}
is a smooth bounded solution of the Navier-Stokes equations. We are interested in how turbulence will develop within a region or domain
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
. The Euclidean norm of the fluid velocity is
\|U_{a}(x,t)\|_{E_{2}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}=(\sum_{a}^{3}U_{a}|(x,t)|^{2})^{1/2}
so that at each
(x,t)
one can define a volume-averaged Reynolds number throughout
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
as
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)=\|{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\|L\nu^{-1}
. Now let
{{\mathscr{R}}}(x)={{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
be a spatial Bargmann-Fock Gaussian random field with expectation
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\rangle=0
and binary correlation

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(y)\big\|\!\big\|_{E_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)~{{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\Sigma}(x,y;\lambda)={\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

(2.107)

where
(\mathsf{C})>0
is a constant. The derivatives of the Bargmann-Fock field
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)
are
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
,
\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x),\newline
\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}_{b}(y,t)

and exist. The field
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
can also be interpreted as a ’smeared out’ white-in-space noise
{\mathscr{W}}(x)
.

Let
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
be a critical Reynold’s number at which the smooth flow makes a (sharp) transition to a turbulent flow. The smooth N-S flow
U_{a}(x,t)
is then ’mixed’ with the random field
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)
to produce a random or turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
which is also a random field. The turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
then grows with the averaged Reynolds number
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
throughout
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
. The following are then proposed:

(a)

The ’mixing of the fields
U_{a}(x,t)
and
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
at any Reynolds number
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)>\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
is denoted by

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{M}}}:\big\{{U}_{a}(x),\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\}\hookrightarrow{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

(2.108)

The tentative ’mixing formula’ ansatz is then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\bigg[{{\psi}}\big(\big|{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c})(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\big)\bigg]{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]\big|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))|\big){\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x))

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{C}}}_{a}(x,t)\equiv U_{a}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t){{\mathscr{R}}}_{a}(x,t){\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}~[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]

(2.109)

where
\mathscr{Q}_{a}(x,t)=1+{\mathscr{R}}_{a}(x,t)=1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
are random vector fields.
Again,
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]
is the indicator or switch function such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}~\big[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\big]=1,~if~\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)>\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})~or~~\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)~{\in}{{\mathcal{S}}}~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}~\big[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\big]=0,~if~\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\leq\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})~or~~\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)~{\notin}{{\mathcal{S}}}

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}=\{set~of~~all~\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)~>~\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\}

(2.110)

Also

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\equiv U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\left(\bigg|\frac{\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(x,t)\|{L}}{\nu}-\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\bigg|\right){\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle\equiv U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\left(\left|\frac{\mathrm{L}}{\nu}\left\|\frac{1}{Vol(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}U_{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)\right\|-\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\right|\right){\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]

(2.111)

and where
{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\geq 1
is an arbitrary constant ’mixing parameter’ which contributes to the relative magnitude of the turbulent term. For a spatio-temporal field
{\mathscr{B}}(x,t)

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\big)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t))

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t))

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{C}}}_{a}(x,t)\equiv U_{a}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t){\mathscr{Q}}_{a}(x,t)

(2.112)

However, from here we will consider only random fields
{\mathscr{B}}(x)
.

(b)

Keeping
(\mathrm{L},{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}})
fixed, the viscosity
\nu
or the averaged Reynolds number
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
at time
t>0
within the domain
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
is then a ’control parameter’ which be adjusted. This is a nonlinear ’mixing’ of the underlying smooth flow
U_{a}(x,t)
with the random BF field. Representations of the transition to turbulence within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
are given in the figures.

Figure 2
.
Transition of a smooth flow
U_{a}(x,t)
to a turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
as
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
exceeds
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})

Figure 3
.
Gradual transition of a smooth flow
U_{a}(x,t)
to a turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
within domain
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
as
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
exceeds
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})

(c)

The total flow is then a sum of contributions from laminar and turbulent flow, with the turbulent contribution increasing or dominating as the Reynolds number and velocity increase and/or the viscosity decreases.

\displaystyle\underbrace{{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)}_{\textbf{total~turbulent~flow}}=\underbrace{U_{a}(x,t)}_{\textbf{laminar~flow}}+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\big|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})|)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)}_{\textbf{random/turbulent~flow}}

(2.113)

and in the limit of high viscosity and/or small
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
near or at
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
, the turbulent term vanishes or becomes very small.

\displaystyle\lim_{\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\rightarrow\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}}\underbrace{{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)}_{total~turbulent~flow}

(2.114)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{U_{a}(x,t)}_{\textbf{laminar~flow}}+\lim_{\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\rightarrow\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}}\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\big|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})|)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)}_{\textbf{random/turbulent flow}}=U_{a}(x,t)

(2.115)

(d)

The random field
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
describes a 3D randomly fluctuating or stochastic ’fluid geometry’ existing within domain
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
. For a constant laminar flow
U_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}=(U,U,U)
, and for a laminar flow along the z-axis for example
U_{a}=(0,0,U)
. Then the turbulent flow is

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U+{\bm{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}}U{{\psi}}\big(\big|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})|)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.116)

(e)

Since
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\rangle=0
. The mean velocity or stochastic averaged flow is then

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})|)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\big|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})|)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=U_{a}(x,t)

(2.117)

(f)

The function is a generic monotone increasing function that increase with Reynolds number and vanishes at the critical Reynolds number. For example, if the function
{{\psi}}
is an exponential then the turbulence would grow exponentially with Reynolds number and vanish at
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}^{(1)}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
so that

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}~U_{a}(x,t)\exp\big(\big|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\big|\big)-1\big)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.118)

For a more realistic fluid
{{\psi}}
might grow as some power law so that

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\big|\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\big)^{\kappa}\bigg\}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.119)

where
\alpha>0
. If the randomness grows as the square root of the difference between the Reynolds number and the critical Reynolds number then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\sqrt{\big|\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\big)}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.120)

Figure 4.
Transition of a smooth flow
U_{a}(x,t)
to a turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
as
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
exceeds
\bm{\mathrm{R}e}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
. The turbulent velocity fluctuates randomly about the mean.

A representation of the evolution of a turbulent flow from a smooth flow as
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
increases in time beyond the critical value

\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
is given in figures 2 and 3. The evolution of a turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
at some
(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\times\infty)
from laminar flow
U_{a}(x,t)
, once
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)>\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
is also illustrated in Figure 3.

Generally, either the deterministic or random contribution will dominate the contribution to the random flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
depending on the values of the variables
({\bm{\bm{\alpha}}},\nu,\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))
. Bargman-Fock random fields with Gaussian correlations are utilised since they are regulated and differentiable and have the useful property that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\rangle=0

(2.121)

This will simplify the derivations of many estimates and computations.

Proposition 2.27
.

Given
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
, define a ’stochastic material derivative’
{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}_{m}
as

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}_{m}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}

(2.122)

Since
U_{a}(x,t)
is a solution of the NS equations then
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
should be a solution of some stochastically averaged NS equations

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\bm{\mathrm{D}}_{m}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\partial}_{t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)+\textit{"new induced terms"}

(2.123)

New terms should arise via stochastic averaging since the NS equations are
nonlinear
. This is explored in detail in
Section 3
.

Remark 2.28
.

An alternative ’mixing’ ansatz is to utilise
Lemma (2.7)
, and take the Reynolds number at each
(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\times[0,\infty)
rather than the volume-averaged Reynolds number, namely
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t)
given by

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t)={\|U_{a}(x,t)\|L}/\nu,~~~(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\times[0,\infty)

(2.124)

where
U_{a}(x,t)
evolves by the Navier-Stokes equations. Then
{{\psi}}:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is a monotone increasing functional
{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c})
which vanishes for

\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t)\leq\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}
, and
{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t_{1}),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c})<{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t_{2}),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c})
if
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t_{1})<\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t_{2})
. The turbulent flow is then the random vector field of the form (2.19)

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.125)

However, now
\nabla_{b}\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t)\neq 0
and
\nabla_{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\mathrm{Re}_{c})\neq 0
. This leads to an extra term when one takes the derivative
\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
and vastly complicates various derivations and estimates that are to be made.

Proposition 2.29
.

The notation can be simplified somewhat by defining

\displaystyle{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\equiv{{\psi}}\big(\big|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))-\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\big|\big)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]

(2.126)

so that

\displaystyle{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})>0,~if~\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)>\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})~or~\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t){\in}{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})=0,~if~\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\leq\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})~or~\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t){\notin}{\mathcal{S}}

Then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{S}})[\bm{\mathrm{R}e}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)]{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.127)

2.7.
Derivatives of the random field
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
and their expectations

Since the derivatives of the BF fields
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
exist then so do the derivatives and expectations of the turbulent velocity flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
.

Lemma 2.30
.

Given the random fluid flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
then the mean or average values within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
are

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}

(2.128)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t)=0

(2.129)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)\neq

(2.130)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t))\big\|\!\big\|_{E_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)

(2.131)

Proof.

The time derivative is

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.132)

For a spatio-temporal BF field
{\mathscr{B}}(x,t)
, there would be an additional term in (2.132)

\displaystyle{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))~\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.133)

Now taking the expectation or stochastic average, the underbraced terms vanish

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)\bigg\langle{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\bigg\rangle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\|\!\big\|_{E_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\|\!\big\|_{E_{1}({\psi})}}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\big\|\!\big\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)

(2.134)

since
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\rangle=0
. Similarly, the stochastic average of the gradient or spatial derivative is

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={D}_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{\bigg\langle{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\bigg\rangle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle\equiv\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbracket{x\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{\mathbf{}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}))\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)

(2.135)

Since the fluid is incompressible then
{{\mathds{E}}}\big\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\rangle=\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t)=0
. The 2nd derivative is

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t;\mathbb{I})\nabla_{a}{D}_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

(2.136)

Again, taking the expectation, all underbraced terms vanish so that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\mathfrak{I\!H})}}+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big)\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle\equiv\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}+\underbracket{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}\underbrace{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}}

(2.137)

∎

Remark 2.31
.

If the ansatz (2.125) was used, then there would be an extra term in the spatial derivative so that

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){\psi}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c})\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.138)

plus three extra terms when one takes the second derivative or Laplacian. This greatly complicates derivations and estimates that are to be made, so the ansatz (2.109) is utilised throughout.

Finally, once can (tentatively) propose a functional derivative of the random field or flow at any
t>0

Proposition 2.32
.

Given the random field
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
, the functional derivatives are

\displaystyle\frac{\delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)}={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.139)

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)^{2}}={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\delta^{2}}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)^{2}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(2.140)

with the expectations

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\left\langle\frac{\delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)}\right\rangle={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

(2.141)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\left\langle\frac{\delta^{2}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)^{2}}\right\rangle={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)\frac{\delta^{2}}{\delta\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)^{2}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

(2.142)

2.8.
Reynolds stresses, stochastic averages and correlations

A crucial issue of central importance in any theory of turbulence is how to define, model or calculate Reynolds stresses and other statistical correlations. However, such averages are difficult to define and formulate rigorously. As previously stated in the introduction, one can utilise either long time averages, volume averages or ensemble averages.
Here, instead, we consider binary correlations of the random fields or turbulent flows
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
and
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)
at different positions
(x,y)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
at any time
t>0
. The expectations or averages are taken with respect to

\mathbb{I\!E}\langle\bullet\rangle
. One can also consider any high-order correlations.

General Proposition 2.33
.

Given the turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\mathscr{T}}_{a}(x,t)
within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
, the following correlations and stochastic averages/means can be computed for scales
\lambda\leq L
if the statistics of the regulated isotropic and homogenous random field
{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x,t)
are known.

(a)

The binary velocity correlations for all
(x,y)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
and
(t,s)\in[0,T]
or
(t,s)\in[0,T]

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,y)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,s)\bigg\rangle

(2.143)

For any triplet
(x,y,\mathbf{z})\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
, the triple velocity correlations

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,y,\mathbf{z})={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,s){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg\rangle

(2.144)

The p-point correlations for points
\{x_{i_{1}},x_{2},t),...,x_{i_{p}}(x,t)\}\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
such that

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{1_{1}...i_{M}}(x_{i_{1}},...,x_{i_{M}})

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{i_{1}}(x_{1},t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{i_{2}}(x_{2},t){{\boxtimes}}...{{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{i_{M-1}}(x_{M},t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{i_{p}}(x_{M},t)\bigg\rangle

(2.145)

(b)

The mean velocity, the rms velocity and the p-order moments

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{2}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}^{2}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\sum_{i=1}^{3}|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)|^{2}\bigg\rangle\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}(x,t)\big\|^{2}

(2.146)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|=\sqrt{{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big({{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\big)}=\sqrt{{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\sum_{i=1}^{3}|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)|^{2}\bigg\rangle}\equiv\sqrt{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big[\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}(x,t)\big\|^{2}\big]}

(2.147)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{p}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}=\left({\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}(x,t)|\big\|^{p}_{SE(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\right)^{1/p}=\left({\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big[\sum_{i=1}^{3}|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)|^{p}\big]\right)^{1/p}

(2.148)

(c)

Structure functions or correlations of the form

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{S}}}_{p}[{{\mathscr{U}}}]=\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x+\mathbf{\ell},t)-{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{E_{p}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}^{p}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x+\mathbf{\ell},t)-{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{p}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x+\mathbf{\ell},t)-{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big|^{p}\bigg\rangle

(2.149)

for all
p\geq 2
.

(d)

Binary correlations among gradients such as

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}^{(x)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}^{(y)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

(2.150)

or when
y=x

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|D{i}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|^{2}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}^{(x)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}^{(x)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

(2.151)

’Off-diagonal’ binary correlation such as

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}^{(y)}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(2.152)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(2.153)

(e)

Stochastic averages of the turbulent energy and enstrophy integrals

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathcal{E}}[{{\mathscr{U}}}]\bigg\rangle\equiv{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|^{2}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)\bigg\rangle

(2.154)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{{\psi}}}[{{\mathscr{U}}}]\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\equiv\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\big\|\!\big\|\nabla^{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|^{2}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla^{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)\bigg\rangle

(2.155)

(f)

The averaged or mean nonlinear convective term of the NS equations

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}({{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t))\bigg\rangle

(2.156)

and the full stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations

\displaystyle\bigg\|\!\bigg\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{b}({\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{U}}_{b}(x,t))-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)-\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{b}({\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{U}}_{b}(x,t))-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)-\nabla_{a}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(2.157)

or for steady state flow

\displaystyle\bigg\|\!\bigg\|\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{b}({{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t))-\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nabla_{a}{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{1}(\mathcal{I\!H})}

\displaystyle\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{b}({{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t))-\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nabla_{a}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(2.158)

3.
The stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations for the turbulent flow I: Neglecting the pressure term

We now present the main theorem, which gives the stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations satisfied by the turbulent fluid flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
.
The stochastic averaging leads to an extra non-vanishing term via the nonlinear convective term in the Navier-Stokes equations. Traditionally, statistical
averaging or Reynolds averaging of NS equations is done with respect to either spatial or ensemble averages as discussed in the introduction. This has physical limitations and can’t be rigorously defined. Here, instead the stochastic averaging or expectations
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\langle\bullet\big\rangle
is done with respect to a well-defined theory of classical random fields, with which the underlying flow is ’mixed’ to produce a turbulent fluid flow. Here, random fluctuations induced within the pressure gradient term
\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)
are ignored for now to minimise complications. There are incorporated in detail in
Section 5
.

Theorem 3.1
.

(
Stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations for which
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
is a solution)

Let
U_{a}(x,t)
be a smooth deterministic flow satisfying the NS equations within a domain
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\bm{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
with
t\in[0,T]
or
t\in[0,\infty)
and
\mathrm{Vol}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\sim L^{3}
. The flow
U_{a}(x,t)
is at least smooth enough to differentiable to 2nd order and satisfying some suitable BCs and initial data on
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
. The fluid has viscosity
\nu
and is compressible so that
\nabla^{a}U_{a}=0
. The (sharp) critical transitional Reynolds number at which a smooth flow starts to become turbulent is
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
. Now let
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
be a Bargman-Fock Gaussian random field existing for all
x\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
and having the statistical properties previously defined so that

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{B}}(x)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y)\bigg\rangle={\Sigma}(x,y;\lambda)={\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{2}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}^{2}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle={\Sigma}(x,x;\lambda)=\mathsf{C}

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

(3.1)

where
\lambda
is the correlation length. Let
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})=\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(x,t)\|\mathrm{L}/\nu>\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}^{(1)}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
for all
(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\times[0,T]
. As before, the smooth flow
U_{a}(x,t)
is ’mixed’ with the BF random field
{\mathscr{B}}(x)
to give a turbulent flow of the form

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

(3.2)

so that the mean or averaged flow is
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\rangle=U_{a}(x,t)
. The pressure fluctuations (for now) are not considered. The turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
is then a solution of the following stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations

\displaystyle\bigg\|\!\bigg\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.3)

where
\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{1}(t),\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{2}(t)
are dimensionless.

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{1}(t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}

(3.4)

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{2}(t)=1+\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{1}(t)

(3.5)

More succinctly, The turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
is a solution of the stochastically averaged NS equations where the nonlinear term is now modified by a factor
\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{2}(t)
.

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+D{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}(t)_{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.6)

Remark 3.2
.

Because there is a nonlinear term in the NS equations, this introduces a correlation between the random field
{\mathscr{U}}^{b}(x,t)
and its derivative

\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
, that is
{\mathscr{U}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
with (nonvanishing) expectation

\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\langle{\mathscr{U}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)\rangle\neq 0
.

Proof.

Substituting
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
into the Navier-Stokes equations and taking the derivatives gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+{\mathscr{U}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+U_{a}(x){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle-\overbracket{\nu\delta^{ij}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\times\underbrace{\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,,t)}_{=0}\times{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\nabla_{a}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U^{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle++{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+U^{b}(x,t)|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)\bigg|{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\bigg|^{2}|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}U^{b}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)+\overbracket{\nabla_{a}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)}

(3.7)

The pieces of the underlying deterministic Navier-Stokes equations are emphasised with an overbracket and the extra terms represent the random field contributions from turbulence. Now taking the stochastic average
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\langle\bullet\big\rangle
,or equivalently the stochastic norm, all underbraced (linear) terms vanish upon using (3.1)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{U_{a}(x){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\overbracket{\nu\delta^{ij}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\times\underbrace{\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,,t)}_{=0}\times{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\underbrace{U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U^{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}+\overbracket{\nabla_{a}{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{x,t})}

(3.8)

This leaves extra non-vanishing terms arising from the non-linear (convective) term of the NS equations so that

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\mathbf{P}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-\overbracket{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)1+{{\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}}}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)

(3.9)

∎

Corollary 3.3
.

In the limit that the volume-averaged Reynolds number
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
over
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
at any
t>0
is reduced to or below the critical Reynolds number
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
, the extra term
\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)
vanishes and
\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)=1
the standard Navier-Stokes PDEs are recovered so that

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\mathbf{P}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t),~~{\forall}~\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\leq\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})

(3.10)

3.1.
Equivalence of the averaged NS equations to a set of transformed equations

The stochastically avergaged NS equations (3.3) can be shown to be equivalent to a set of transformed NS equations

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
U_{a}(x,t)
be a fluid flow with viscosity
\nu
evolving according to the NS equations from initial data, for all
{\mathbb{R}}\times[0,\infty)
.
Let
\bm{\mathfrak{U}}(t)
be the volume-averaged velocity within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
so that the averaged Reynolds number
anywhere within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
at any
t\in[0,\infty)
is
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)=\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(x,t)\|\mathrm{L}/\nu
, and as before

\mathrm{Vol}[\bm{\mathfrak{D}}]\sim\mathrm{L}^{3}
. Define the following ’boost’ transform
\bm{\mathfrak{B}}:\mathbb{R}^{(+)}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{(+)}
on the velocity and pressure such that

\displaystyle U_{a}(x,t)\hookrightarrow{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)U_{a}(x,t)={\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hookrightarrow\frac{\partial}{\partial t}-{{\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bm{\mathfrak{B}}(t)}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}(t)}}}={\mathbf{D}}_{t}

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}\hookrightarrow\nabla_{b}

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)\hookrightarrow{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\mathbf{P}(x,t)={\mathfrak{P}}(x,t)

(3.11)

where
{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)
is a smooth function with derivative
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)
. The boosted Navier-Stokes equations then have the form

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(3.12)

so that the non-linear convective term is boosted or rescaled by a factor
{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)
. The averaged Reynolds number
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
is also boosted by a factor
{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)
so that

\displaystyle{\Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)={\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)={\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(t)\|\mathrm{L}\nu^{-1}

(3.13)

Proof.

The transformed or ’boosted’ NS equations are

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}_{t}{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}{\bm{\mathfrak{P}}}(x,t)=0

(3.14)

which upon applying (3.11) become

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}

\displaystyle-{\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)}{{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)}}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)U_{a}(x,t)-\nu{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\big)U_{a}(x,t)+(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)){\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)-(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)){\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)-\nu{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(3.15)

Dividing out by
{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(3.16)

∎

The next theorem establishes that the stochastically averaged NS equations are equivalent to a set of ’boosted’ or transformed deterministic NS equations.

Theorem 3.5
.

Let the scenario and conditions of previous theorems/lemmas hold for a turbulent fluid flow of the form

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)=U_{a}(x)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{a}(x,t)

(3.17)

where
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\langle{\mathscr{B}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{\mathscr{B}}(x,t)\big\rangle={\mathsf{C}}
. The Reynolds number throughout
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
at any
t>0
is
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
and the critical Reynolds number is
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
. As before,
{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})
is an arbitrary monotone increasing function and vanishes at
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)=\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
. Then the stochastically averaged NS equations

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t;{\mathbb{I}}){\mathsf{C}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.18)

are equivalent to the NS equations which will arise from the linear boost transformations of the form

\displaystyle U_{a}(x,t)\longrightarrow{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)U_{a}(x,t)={\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle=1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}~~{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}U_{a}(x,t)=1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\longrightarrow\frac{\partial}{\partial t}-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bm{\mathfrak{B}}(t)}{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}(t)}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{\mathsf{C}}^{2}~{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}^{2}}{1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{\mathsf{C}}^{2}~{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}^{2}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)}{1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)}

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)\longrightarrow{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}(t)\mathbf{P}(x,t)={\bm{\mathfrak{P}}}(x,t)=1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{\mathsf{C}}^{2}~{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}^{2}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\mathbf{P}(x,t)\equiv\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.19)

In the limit as
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)\rightarrow\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
one has

\displaystyle U_{a}(x,t)\hookrightarrow U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hookrightarrow\frac{\partial}{\partial t}

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}\hookrightarrow\nabla_{b}

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.20)

Proof.

The transformed or ’boosted’ NS equations are

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}_{t}{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathfrak{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}{\bm{\mathfrak{P}}}(x,t)=0

(3.21)

which upon applying (3.17) become

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}

\displaystyle-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}}{1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}}1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)

\displaystyle+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\big)U_{a}(x,t)}+(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t))1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}U_{a}(x,t)}-\nu 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\Delta U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t))1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}-\nu 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\Delta U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.22)

Dividing out by
1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(3.23)

which is exactly (3.3).
Equivalently

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}

\displaystyle-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)}{1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t))}1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t))U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu 1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t))^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)

\displaystyle+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\big)U_{a}(x,t)}+(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t))1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)U_{a}(x,t)}-\nu 1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t))1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)-\nu 1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(3.24)

Dividing out by
1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(3.25)

∎

3.2.
Stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations for a turbulent fluid flow arisingaleg from a steady state or laminar flow

Theorem 3.6
.

As before, let
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
contain a fluid of viscosity
\nu
with constant underlying laminar velocity

U_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}=(0,0,U)
throughout
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
when the Reynolds number is at or below the critical value
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}^{(1)}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
. The domain has volume
\mathrm{Vol}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\sim\mathrm{L}^{3}
. As before, the volume-averaged Reynolds number is homogenous or constant through out
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
so that
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})=\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}\|\mathrm{L}/\nu
. The turbulent fluid flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
for any
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})>\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}^{(1)}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
is again the random field

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle=U_{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\equiv U_{a}+{\mathscr{Q}}_{a}(x,t)

(3.26)

so that for very small
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
below the critical value

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\sim U_{a}

(3.27)

where the regulated BF random field
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
has the usual properties

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0,~\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0

(3.28)

Then the turbulent flow
\mathcal{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
is a solution of the stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}_{t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(3.29)

\displaystyle\equiv\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

(3.30)

Proof.

The derivatives of the field
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
are

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

(3.31)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){{{\psi}}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\Delta{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

(3.32)

since
\nabla_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})=0
, and
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})=0
for a steady state flow. Then the nonlinear convective
term is simply

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}U_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}U^{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})~{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

(3.33)

with expectation

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\bigg\rangle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}U^{b}|{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(3.34)

The SA Navier-Stokes equations are then

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}_{m}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle-\nu{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\bigg]\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\Delta{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+U^{b}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\bigg]\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+U_{a}U^{b}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(3.35)

\displaystyle=U^{b}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle-\nu U^{b}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\Delta{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(3.36)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}U_{a}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}U^{b}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

∎

4.
Binary and triple velocity correlations

The next theorems deal with estimates for the binary, triple and high-order velocity correlations for the turbulent fluid flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
.

Theorem 4.1
.

As before, let
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\bm{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, with
\mathrm{Vol}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\sim L^{3}
be the region where the random flow occurs so that for all
x\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
and
t\in[0,\infty)
the turbulent flow is the random field
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{{\psi}}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)
, where
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x)\equiv\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})=\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(x,t)\|\mathrm{L}/\nu
is the ensemble or spatially averaged Reynolds number in
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
. The Bargman-Fock random field
{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)
has the usual Gaussian distribution and correlation properties so that for any
(x,y,\mathbf{z})\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
the expectation vanishes
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\rangle=\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x,t)\big\rangle=\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\big\rangle=0
and the following binary correlations hold with Gaussian decay and correlation length scale
\lambda\leq L
.

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bm{\bigg\langle}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathsf{\Sigma}}(x,y;\lambda)=\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-y\|\lambda^{-2})

(4.1)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(z,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathsf{\Sigma}}(x,\mathbf{z};\lambda)={\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

(4.2)

\displaystyle\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathsf{\Sigma}}(y,\mathbf{z};\lambda)={\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

(4.3)

For
(x,y,\mathbf{z})\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
then the binary and triple velocity correlations are defined as the Reynolds tensors.

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)=\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(4.4)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,y,\mathbf{z})=\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\equiv{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg\rangle

(4.5)

with the 2nd and 3rd-order moments being the tensors

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x)=\big\|\!\big\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}=\lim_{y\rightarrow x}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{y\hookrightarrow x}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,y)=\lim_{y\rightarrow x}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

(4.6)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x)=\bigg\|\!\bigg\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{1}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle=\lim_{y\rightarrow x}={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{(z,y)\rightarrow x}\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ijk}(x,y)=\lim_{(z,y)\rightarrow x}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{{\boxtimes}}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(4.7)

(a)

Then the binary velocity correlations are:

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)_{ij}(x,y)=\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)\left(1+{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})\right)

(4.8)

and

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)=\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\lim_{y\uparrow x}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{y\uparrow x}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\left(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{y\uparrow x}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\left(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}\right)

(4.9)

(b)

The triple correlations are

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,y,\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(z,t)\bigg\rangle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)

\displaystyle+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\times U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

(4.10)

(c)

For
y\rightarrow x
and
\mathbf{z}\rightarrow x

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x)

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\times\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)1+3{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\mathsf{C}

(4.11)

Proof.

The turbulent flows at
x
and
y
are the random fields

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t))

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t)=U_{a}(y,t)+\times{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t))

(4.12)

The product of the fields is then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)

(4.13)

Taking the stochastic expectation then give a Reynolds-type tensor, with underbraced terms vanishing

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,y)

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|{\xi}^{-2})

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\bigg]^{2}{\mathsf{\Sigma}}(x,y;\lambda)

(4.14)

Then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x)=\lim_{x\hookrightarrow y}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

(4.15)

The averaged NS equations can then be expressed in terms of this stress tensor.

Lemma 4.2
.

The averaged NS equations are

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\mathbf{P}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

(4.16)

Using (4.15) this is then

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)+\nabla^{b}{\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{ij}(x)

(4.17)

In terms of the random vector field
\mathscr{Q}_{a}(x,t)
, the turbulent flow is

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\equiv U_{a}(x,t)+{\mathscr{Q}}_{a}(x,t)

(4.18)

If we write
\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ij}(x,x)=\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{Q}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{Q}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle
then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}{\mathbf{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t)+\nabla^{b}\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ij}(x,x)

(4.19)

Hence, the extra term which is induced can be expressed as a Reynolds-type stress tensor.

4.1.
Triple velocity correlations

For the triple velocity correlations for any
(x,y,\mathbf{z})\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
, the random/turbulent flows are the random fields

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t))

(4.20)

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t)=U_{a}(y,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(y,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t))

(4.21)

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(\mathbf{z},t)=U_{a}(\mathbf{z},t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t))

(4.22)

The product of these random fields is then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(z,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)

(4.23)

Taking the expectation or average, then all underbraced terms vanish giving the correlation tensor

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ij}(x,

\displaystyle y,\mathbf{z})=\bm{{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(z,t)\bigg\rangle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\bm{{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(z,t)}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)\bigg\rangle}~

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg]{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\bm{{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t)\bigg\rangle
