\displaystyle+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-(|x-y|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle+\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)\bigg|{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-(|x-\mathbf{z}|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle+\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{3}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-(|y-\mathbf{z}|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\equiv U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)1+\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{2}\big|{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times{\mathsf{C}}\big(\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})+\exp(-\|x-z\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}+\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}\big)

\displaystyle\equiv{{\mathsf{R}}}_{ijk}(x,y,\mathbf{z}1+\bm{\bm{\alpha}}^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathsf{C}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times\mathsf{C}\big(\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})+\exp(-\|x-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}+\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}\big)

(4.24)

∎
The turbulent contributions to the binary and triple velocity correlations then vanish at large separations
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
and/or low Reynolds numbers
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t))<\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
, and the flow becomes smooth or laminar again.

Corollary 4.3
.

The turbulent contributions to
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}(x,y)
and
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}(x,y,\mathbf{z})
vanish

(a)

If\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
then the tensors decay rapidly due to the Gaussian.

(b)

If
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)<\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
for all
t>0
or some
t=t^{\prime}
or some
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
.

(c)

If the viscosity
\mu
tends to infinity or becomes very high.

For
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
then
\exp(-\|x-y\|\lambda^{-2})=0
the binary correlation becomes

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ij}(x,y)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)(1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\Sigma}(x,y;\lambda)

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)(1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}\exp(-|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(x,t)\equiv{\bm{\mathrm{R}}}_{ij}(x,y)

(4.25)

For
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
but
\|x-\mathbf{z}\|,\|y-\mathbf{z}\|\leq\lambda
then the triple correlation becomes

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ijk}(x,y,\mathbf{z})=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times{\Sigma}(x,y;\lambda)+{\Sigma}(x,\mathbf{z};\lambda)+{\Sigma}(y,\mathbf{z};\lambda)\big)

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\big\}^{3}

\displaystyle\times\mathsf{C}\big(\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})+\exp(-\|x-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}+\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}+\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}\big)

(4.26)

and so on. If
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
and also
\|x-\mathbf{z}\|\gg\lambda
and
\|y-\mathbf{z}\|\gg\lambda
then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(\mathbf{x,y,z})=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{3}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}

\displaystyle\times{\mathsf{C}}\big(\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})+\exp(-\|x-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}+\exp(-\|y-\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\lambda^{-2}\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(\mathbf{z},t)

(4.27)

If
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)<\mathrm{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
for any
t>0
then the volume-averaged Reynolds number over
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
falls below the critical Reynolds value and so
{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))=0
. This indicates that the flow returns to being laminar or nonturbulent so that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathsf{T}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,y})=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}\exp(-|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(x,t)

(4.28)

and similarly for
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(\mathbf{x,y,z})
and all high-order correlations. Finally, since
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)=\frac{\|\mathfrak{A}_{a}(x,t)\|L}{\mu}
where
Vol[\bm{\mathfrak{D}}]\sim\mathrm{L}^{3}
and
\|\bm{\mathfrak{U}}_{a}(x,t)
is the volume-averaged velocity within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
, then
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)<\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
for sufficiently large
\mu
. Also
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)=\frac{\|\mathfrak{Q}_{a}(x,t)\|L}{\mu}\rightarrow 0
as

\mu\rightarrow\infty
. Hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\mu\rightarrow\infty}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,y})=U_{a}(x)U_{b}(y)

(4.29)

\displaystyle\lim_{\mu\rightarrow\infty}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(\mathbf{x,y,z})=U_{a}(x)U_{b}(y)U_{k}(\mathbf{z})

(4.30)

Lemma 4.4
.

The time evolution of
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,y};\lambda)
is

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,y};t)

\displaystyle=2U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)|{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){\partial}_{t}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){\Sigma}(x,y;\lambda)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t))U_{b}(y,t)+({\partial}_{t}U_{b}(x,t))U_{b}(y,t)

\displaystyle\times 1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\mathsf{\Sigma}}(\mathbf{x,y};\lambda)

\displaystyle\equiv+2U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)|{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})

(4.31)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){\mathsf{C}}\exp(-|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle+{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t))U_{b}(y,t)+({\partial}_{t}U_{b}(x,t))U_{b}(y,t)

\displaystyle\times 1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})

so that
{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,y};t)=0
if
U_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}=consts
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})=0

The time evolution of all higher-order terms is also readily estimated.

4.2.
Nth-order moments and non-blowup of the turbulent flow

It is necessary and important that the turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
does not blow up for any
t>0
and
x\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
. This means that
the N-th order moments
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\big\langle\big|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\big|^{N}\big\rangle
are bounded and finite so that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg\rangle\equiv\bigg\|\!\bigg\|{{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|_{SE_{N}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}<\infty

(4.33)

or

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg\rangle\equiv\bigg\|\!\bigg\|{{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|_{V_{r}(\mathcal{C}}<K

(4.34)

The
Nth
-order moments
{\bm{\mathfrak{E}}}\langle|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)|^{N}\rangle
or can be estimated as follows

Theorem 4.5
.

Given the turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
as previously defined, the Nth-order moments are then

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg\rangle\equiv\bigg\|\!\bigg\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|^{N}_{E_{N}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))}

\displaystyle=\sum_{M=1}^{N}\binom{N}{M}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{N-M}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{M}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathfrak{F}})\bigg]^{M}\big[\tfrac{1}{2}({\mathsf{C}}+(-1)^{M}{\mathsf{C}})\big]

(4.35)

Then the moments of the turbulent fluid flow are bounded and do not blow up for all
x\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
and
t>0
.

\displaystyle\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg\rangle\equiv\bigg\|\!\bigg\|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|^{N}_{E_{N}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}<\infty

(4.36)

for all
(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}[0\infty)
iff
\|U_{a}(x,t\|^{N}<\infty
. Hence, the turbulent flow is finite and bounded provided that the underlying NS flow

U_{a}(x,t)
is finite and bounded.

Proof.

Using the binomial expansion

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{p}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\bigg|U_{a}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\sum_{M=0}^{N}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{N}}\big|U_{a}(x,t)\big|^{N-M}\bigg|U_{a}(x,t)\mathbf{M}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg|^{m}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\sum_{M=0}^{N}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{M}}\big|U_{a}(x,t)\big|^{N-M}|\mathbf{M}|^{M}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{M}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{M}\bigg|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg|^{M}|\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\sum_{M=0}^{N}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{M}}\big|U_{a}(x,t)\big|^{N}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{M}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{M}\bigg|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg|^{M}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\sum_{M=0}^{N}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{M}}\big|U_{a}(x,t)\big|^{N}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{M}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{M}\bigg|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg|^{M}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{M=0}^{N}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{M}}\big|U_{a}(x,t)\big|^{N}|\mathbf{M}|^{M}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{M}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg|^{M}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{M=0}^{M}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{M}}\big|U_{a}(x,t)\big|^{p}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{q}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{M}\bigg\|\!\bigg\|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\|\!\bigg\|^{M}_{E_{M}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{M=1}^{N}\binom{\mathrm{N}}{\mathrm{M}}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{N}|\mathbf{M}|^{M}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\big[\tfrac{1}{2}({\mathsf{C}}+(-1)^{M}{\mathsf{C}})\bigg]

(4.37)

∎

Corollary 4.6
.

As a corollary, the equation for the 2nd-order moments follow for
p=2
.

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\big|{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{2}\bigg\rangle=\sum_{q=1}^{2}\binom{2}{q}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{q}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{q}\left[\frac{1}{2}({\mathsf{C}}+(-1)^{q}{\mathsf{C}})\right]

\displaystyle=\binom{2}{0}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}\mathsf{C}+\binom{2}{1}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\left({{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\right){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\bigg|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg|^{2}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\binom{2}{2}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\bigg]^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\binom{2}{0}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}+\binom{2}{2}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\bigg]^{2}\mathsf{C}

\displaystyle=|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}+|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}

\displaystyle=|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}\left(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}\bigg[{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\bigg]^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\right)

(4.38)

which is exactly (4.15).

5.
Stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations for the turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
II-incorporation of the pressure gradient term

The averaged Navier-Stokes equations are considered again, but now incorporating the random fluctuations induced within the pressure gradient term
\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)
. The turbulent velocity
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
will induce a ’turbulent pressure’
\mathscr{P}(x,t)
. We begin with the
NS equations, defined on
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)-\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathrm{P}(x,t)=0,~~x\in\mathcal{I\!H},t>0

(5.1)

with
\nabla_{b}U^{b}(\mathrm{x},t)=0
and
\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0
and
\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0
on
\partial\mathcal{Q}
. Taking the divergence
\nabla^{a}
gives

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t)-\nabla^{a}\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla^{a}U^{b}(\mathrm{x},t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla^{a}\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle\Delta\mathbf{P}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))=0

(5.2)

which is

\displaystyle-\Delta\mathbf{P}(x,t)=\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))

(5.3)

Any two solutions of (5.3) can only differ by a harmonic function. Formally, the solution will be of the form

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)=(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))

(5.4)

Lemma 5.1
.

The pressure gradient and Navier-Stokes equations can be expressed in the form

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{U}_{a}(y,t){U}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta_{y}U_{a}(y,t)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

(5.5)

The Navier-Stokes equations can then be written as

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)-\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+{U}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{U}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbf{I\!H}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta_{y}U_{a}(y,t)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y\right|=0

(5.7)

Proof.

The formal solution of the Laplace equation for the pressure is [REF]

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)=(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))

(5.8)

where
-\Delta)^{-1}
is the integral operator.

\displaystyle(-\Delta)^{-1}{f}(x,t)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{{f}(y,t)d^{3}y}{|x-y|}

(5.9)

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{1}{|x-y|}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{U}_{a}(y,t){U}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

(5.10)

The gradient is then

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}^{(x)}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\nabla_{a}^{x}\left(\frac{1}{|x-y|}\right))\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{U}_{a}(y,t){U}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{U}_{a}(y,t){U}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta_{y}U_{a}(y,t)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

(5.11)

and the form (5.7) of the N-S equations follows.
∎

Note that if
(-\Delta)^{-1}
and
\nabla_{a}
are considered in the Fourier space then they respectively correspond to multiplication by

(2\pi|\mathbf{k}|)^{-2}
and
2\pi i
so that they respectively commute. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(x,t)=(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\equiv\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}(-\Delta)^{-1}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)=\mathbf{T}_{ij}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)

(5.12)

On the whole space it can also be shown that (REF) the following bounds hold. For all
0<r<\infty

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}(\bullet,t)\|_{L_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq{\mathrm{C}}_{r}\|U_{a}(\bullet,t)\|^{2}_{L_{2r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(\bullet,t)\|_{L_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq{\mathrm{C}}_{r}\|U^{b}(\bullet,t)\nabla_{a})U_{b}(\bullet,t)\|_{L_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(5.14)

These are proved via the Calderon-Zygmond Theorem (REF).

Turbulence in the fluid velocity will induce turbulence or random fluctuations in the pressure or the gradient of the pressure via the nonlinear term

Lemma 5.2
.

Given the integral representation of
\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(\mathrm{x},t)
, consider a turbulent flow at
y\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
at time
t>0
, again of the form

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t)=U_{a}(\mathrm{y},t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(\mathrm{y},t){{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})){{\mathscr{B}}}(y)

(5.15)

Then the turbulent velocity induces a turbulent pressure gradient
\nabla_{a}\mathscr{P}(x,t)
with expectation

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\mathscr{P}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{a}\mathscr{P}(x,t)\big(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}))|^{2}\mathsf{C}\big)

(5.16)

Proof.

The pressure gradient is

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}^{(x)}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\nabla_{a}^{x}\left(\frac{1}{|x-y|}\right))\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{U}_{a}(y,t){U}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{U}_{a}(y,t){U}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

Hence if
U_{a}(x,t)\rightarrow{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}^{(x)}{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

(5.17)

Taking the stochastic expectation

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}^{(x)}{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle d^{3}y

\displaystyle\equiv=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle d^{3}y

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(y,t)d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}\times U_{a}(y,t)U_{b}(y,t)+U_{a}(y,t)U_{b}(y,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla_{b}\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}(\nabla_{b}\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(y,t))U_{b}(y,t)+U_{a}(y,t)\underbrace{\nabla^{b}U_{b}(y,t)}\big)|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}^{2}\mathsf{C}\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla_{b}\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla_{b}\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t),\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla_{b}\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla_{b}\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}\mathscr{P}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}

(5.18)

∎

Theorem 5.3
.

Stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equations with induced pressure fluctuations
.
Let
U_{a}(x,t)
satisfy the Navier-Stokes equations within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
so that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\left|\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta_{y}U_{a}(y,t)U_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y\right|

(5.19)

and as before
\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)=0
for an incompressible fluid. For
\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)>\mathbf{Re}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
then the flow becomes turbulent so that as before the turbulent flow is the random field

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(5.20)

The turbulent flow then satisfies the following stochastically averaged Navier-Stokes equation

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+D{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv{\mathds{E}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+{\mathscr{U}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta_{y}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y\right|\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(5.21)

More succinctly, the turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
is a solution of the stochastically averaged N-S equations where the nonlinear and pressure gradient terms are now modified by the factor
\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}_{2}

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+D{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(5.22)

Proof.

Substituting
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
into the Navier-Stokes equations and taking the derivatives gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+{\mathscr{U}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+U_{a}(x){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle-\overbracket{\nu\delta^{ij}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\times\underbrace{\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,,t)}_{=0}\times{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle-\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})\nabla_{a}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U^{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle++{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+U^{b}(x,t)|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)\bigg|{{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})\bigg|^{2}|{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}U^{b}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{U}}_{b}(y,t)d^{3}y

(5.23)

The pieces of the underlying deterministic Navier-Stokes equations are emphasised with an overbracket and the extra terms represent the random field contributions from turbulence. Now taking the stochastic average
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\langle\bullet\big\rangle
,or equivalently the stochastic norm, all underbraced (linear) terms vanish upon using (3.1)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}\bm{\mathrm{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{U_{a}(x){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\overbracket{\nu\delta^{ij}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\times\underbrace{\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,,t)}_{=0}\times{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\underbrace{U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U^{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\nabla^{a}_{(y)}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle d^{3}y

(5.24)

Taking only the expectation of the last term first and using (5.18)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}{\mathscr{P}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{U_{a}(x){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\overbracket{\nu\delta^{ij}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\times\underbrace{\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,,t)}_{=0}\times{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nu\delta^{ij}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\underbrace{U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U^{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)d^{3}y

(5.25)

Taking the remaining stochastic expectations leaves only one more additional term involving
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\langle{\mathscr{B}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{B}}(x)\rangle=\mathsf{C}
, with all other (linear) terms vanishing so that

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}^{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}\mathscr{P}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-\overbracket{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)\delta^{ij}d^{3}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)d^{3}y

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-\overbracket{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}\frac{(x_{a}-y_{a})}{|x-y|^{2}}\Delta\big(U_{a}(y,t)\big)U_{b}(y,t)\big)d^{3}y\

\displaystyle=\overbracket{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-\overbracket{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\overbracket{\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\mathsf{C}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(x,t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}{\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle={\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}{U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}

\displaystyle+1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\mathsf{C}{\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle={\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+1+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle\equiv{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

\displaystyle\equiv{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)}-{\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){U^{b}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)}+{\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)}

(5.26)

∎

6.
Homogeneity and isotropy

A turbulent flow is essentially homogenous and isotropic (HAI) if it is invariant under translations and rotations in space. However, this is an idealisation and real turbulence is generally not HAI. Boundaries and other physical constraints result in spatial variations of the flow properties. Turbulent flows are also not generally isotropic since any preferred flow direction–for example, along the z-axis of a pipe or tube– is simply not compatible with the concept of isotropy. However, for large-scale turbulence or turbulent flows in the ocean and atmosphere, there can be regions where turbulence is HAI to a good approximation and there can be scales much less than the physical scale of the system where local homogeneity and isotropy holds. A laboratory idealization of HAI turbulence can be created by passing a laminar or uniform flow through a grid or mesh. The turbulence which forms downstream of the mesh is essentially HAI. However, HAI turbulence is somewhat of a ’manmade’ lab idealisation and is generally not realized in nature.

From (1.2)-(1.4) the traditional definition of HAI turbulence is that the derivative of the binary velocity correlation tensor at a point should vanish so that

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}\bm{R}_{ij}(x,t)=\nabla^{b}\bigg\langle\widetilde{U_{a}(x,t)}\widetilde{U_{b}(x,t)}\bigg\rangle=0

(6.1)

and for any
\ell>0

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\widetilde{U_{a}(x+\mathbf{\ell},t)}\widetilde{U_{b}(x+\mathbf{\ell},t)}\bigg\rangle=\widetilde{\bigg\langle U_{a}(x,t)}\widetilde{U_{b}(x,t)}\bigg\rangle

(6.2)

where
\big\langle\bullet\big\rangle
is the spatial, temporal or ensemble average as discussed in the introduction. Instead, we utilise the following definitions of HAI for the
turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
with respect to the stochastic average or expectation
\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}\langle\bullet\rangle
.

Definition 6.1
.

A smooth Navier-Stokes flow will be said to be isotropic if
U_{a}(x,t)
is isotropic for all
(x,t)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\times[0,T]
if

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t)=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)

(6.3)

Proposition 6.2
.

The turbulent flow is incompressible if

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

(6.4)

which is the case if the underlying NS flow is incompressible
\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t)=0
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))\nabla^{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(6.5)

Taking the stochastic expectation

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),\mathbb{I}){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}=\nabla^{a}U_{a}(x,t)

(6.6)

since the underbraced terms vanish. So the turbulent flow is incompressible if the underlying NS flow is incompressible.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

The turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
is
isotropic
if

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(6.7)

which is the case if
\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t)=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)
; that is, if the underlying Navier-Stokes flow is isotropic.

Proof.

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(6.8)

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)=\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(6.9)

The stochastic expectations are then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

(6.10)

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{b}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle}

(6.11)

and the underbraced terms vanish since
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0
and
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle=0
. Then
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

iff
\nabla_{a}U_{b}(x,t)=\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)

∎

Proposition 6.4
.

A turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
is HAI if the following hold:

(a)

The isotropy condition

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{a}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(6.12)

(b)

The derivative of the Reynolds-type stress tensors vanishes to all orders.

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,x};t)=\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

(6.13)

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(\mathbf{x,x,x};t)=\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

(6.14)

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{i_{1}...i_{N}}(x...x;t)=\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\prod_{\alpha=1}^{N}{{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{U}}_{i_{\alpha}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=0

(6.15)

and this holds if the underlying NS flow is laminar so that
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}=const.

Proof.

Equation (6.12) is true from Proposition 6.3. The derivative is

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,x;t)=\nabla^{b}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t))U_{b}(x,t)+(\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t))U_{a}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t))U_{b}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

(6.16)

since
\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)=0
from incompressibility. This is zero iff
\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)=0
or
when
U_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}=const.
Similarly for the 3-order tensor

{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(\mathbf{x,x,x},;^{\prime}t)

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,x;t)=\nabla^{b}\big((U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\big)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}\bigg|(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})^{3}{\mathsf{C}}

(6.17)

Similarly, for the derivative of the 3rd-order tensor

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,x,x;t)=\nabla^{b}\big(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\big)

\displaystyle 1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{3}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{3}{\mathsf{C}}^{3}\mathsf{C}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{b}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))U_{k}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla^{b}U_{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{3}{\mathsf{C}}^{3}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=(\nabla^{b}(U_{a}(x,t))U_{b}(x,t))U_{k}(x,t)+\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)U_{a}U_{k}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla^{b}U_{k}(x,t)

(6.18)

\displaystyle\times 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{3}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=(\nabla^{b}(U_{a}(x,t))U_{b}(x,t))U_{k}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla^{b}U_{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{3}{\mathsf{C}}

(6.19)

which equals zero if
U_{b}(x,t)=U_{b}
=const. Finally, the derivative of the Nth-order tensor is given by the binomial expansion

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{i_{1}...i_{N}}(x...x;t)=\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\prod_{\alpha=1}^{N}{{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{U}}_{i_{\alpha}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\nabla^{b}\sum_{M=1}^{N}\binom{N}{M}\bigg|U_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg|\bigg|{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{M}\big[\frac{1}{2}({\mathsf{C}}+(-1)^{M}{\mathsf{C}}\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{M=1}^{N}\binom{N}{M}\nabla^{b}\bigg|U_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N}\bigg|{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{M}\big[\frac{1}{2}{\mathsf{C}}+(-1)^{M}{\mathsf{C}}\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{M=1}^{N}\binom{N}{M}N\bigg|U_{a}(x,t)\bigg|^{N-1}\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}))^{M}\big[\frac{1}{2}({\mathsf{C}}+(-1)^{M}{\mathsf{C}}\big]

which again is zero iff
\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)=0
or
U_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}=const.
.
∎

7.
Differential equation for
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)

We now seek a differential equation for the evolution of the Reynolds-like stress

{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)
. First, the following preliminary lemma establishes the differential equation of the evolution
of
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)

Lemma 7.1
.

Given the tensor
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)
, where
U_{a}(x,t)

evolves by the N-S equations, then the tensor
\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{ij}(x,t)
evolves as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathrm{R}}}_{ij}(x,t)+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(7.1)

Proof.

Since
U_{a}(x,t)
satisfies the N-S equations for all
x\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t>0
,then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+\nabla_{a}P(x,t)=0

(7.2)

Multiplying though on the lhs by
U_{b}(x,t)
gives

\displaystyle U_{b}(x,t)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu U_{b}(x,t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)=0

(7.3)

Then

\displaystyle U_{b}(x,t){\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)=\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))

(7.4)

\displaystyle\Delta(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t))=U_{a}(x,t)\Delta U_{b}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+2\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)

\displaystyle=U(x,t)\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)=U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)\equiv U_{b}(x,t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)

(7.5)

\displaystyle\nabla_{b}(U_{j(x,t)}U^{b}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t))\equiv\nabla_{b}\big(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\delta^{kj}(U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{k}+U_{a}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{b}(x,t)\big)

\displaystyle=\delta^{kj}U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla^{k}U_{k}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{b}(x,t)

\displaystyle=U_{b}(x,t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)

(7.6)

using
\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)=0
and
\nabla_{b}U_{b}(x,t)\equiv\delta^{ij}\delta_{ij}\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)=0
. Then

\displaystyle U_{b}(x,t)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)-\nu U_{b}(x,t)\Delta U_{a}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}\big(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\big)-\nu\Delta\big(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\big)+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}\big(U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)\big)+U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}P(x,t

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)+U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}P(x,t)=0

(7.7)

∎

Given (7.1), one can also find a DE for the Reynolds-like stress tensor
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)
.

Theorem 7.2
.

Given the turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
, then the tensor

{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)=\big\langle{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{\mathscr{U}}_{b}(x,t)\big\rangle
satisfies the following PDE

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{J}}_{2}(t){\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)+2{\mathcal{J}}(t){\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\-\nu\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)+{\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}}_{3}(t)U_{b}(x,t)U^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{a}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)

(7.8)

Proof.

Beginning with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(7.9)

replace
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)
with
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{U}}_{b}(x,t)
so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)

\displaystyle+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)+{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(7.10)

and take the expectation so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(x,t)\bigg\rangle+{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)-\nu\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)+\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)\nabla_{a}\mathbf{P}(x,t)

(7.11)

Now

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})\big)^{3}{\mathsf{C}}

(7.12)

The derivatives are:

(a)

The derivative of the stress tensor

\displaystyle\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\big(\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)\big)U_{b}(x,t)+(\underbrace{\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t))}U_{a}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{b}U_{a}(x,t)\big)U_{b}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})

\displaystyle=\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{B}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

(7.13)

where the underbraced term vanishes due to the incompressibility of the fluid.

(b)

The Laplacian of the stress tensor

\displaystyle\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)\equiv\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\big(\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}U^{a}\big)U_{b}(x,t)+\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)\underbrace{\nabla^{b}U_{b}(x,t)}\times 1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\big(\nabla_{b}\nabla^{b}U^{a}\big)U_{b}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\Delta{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

(7.14)

(c)

The derivative of the triple tensor is

\displaystyle\delta^{kj}\nabla_{b}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)=\delta^{kj}\big(\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t))\big)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle\times 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\bm{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{kj}\big(\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t))\big)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{k}(x,t)\delta^{kj}\times 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{kj}\big(\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t))\big)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)\underbrace{\nabla_{b}U^{b}(x,t)}\times 1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{kj}\big(\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t))\big)U_{b}(x,t)U_{k}(x,t)U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)

\displaystyle\times\left(1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}\equiv{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ijk}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t),{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\bm{\mathsf{C}}

(7.15)

(d)

Finally the time derivative is

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{a}(x,t)\big)U_{b}(x,t)+\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}U_{b}(x,t)\big)U_{a}(x,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}|{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle+U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(x,t)2{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}){\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)1+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}+{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}_{ij}(x,t)2{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}){\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})

(7.16)

If substitute and set

\displaystyle\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{{\psi}}}}_{3}(t)={\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{3}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{3}{\mathsf{C}}

(7.17)

then (7.8) follows.

∎

7.1.
Averaged energy integrals

The energy integral is defined as follows.

Definition 7.3
.

As before, let
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
and let
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
contain a fluid of viscosity
\nu
and velocity

U_{a}(x,t)
satisfying the NS equations. The energy integral with respect to domain
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
is then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}[U_{a}(x,t)]=\frac{1}{2}{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}d\mu(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})\equiv\frac{1}{2}{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}d^{3}x\equiv\|U_{a}(\bullet,t)\|_{L_{2}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})}

(7.18)

The energy integral is taken to be bounded so that
\exists\bm{\mathfrak{B}}
such that

{\mathcal{E}}[U_{a}(x,t)]\leq\bm{\mathfrak{B}}
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}|U_{a}(x,t)|^{2}d\mu(x)\leq{\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}

(7.19)

Suppose we now consider the energy bound on a turbulent flow
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
. Then the averaged energy integral for this turbulent flow should be also be bounded.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
be a turbulent flow for
x\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
and
t>0
, as previously defined. Then the averaged energy integral is

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle d\mu(x)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{E}}\big[U_{a}(x,t)\big]1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{\mathsf{C}}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\equiv\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){\mathcal{E}}[U_{a}(x,t)]

(7.20)

so that the energy integral is boosted or re-scaled by a factor
\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t)
.

Proof.

Given the turbulent flow or random field
{\mathscr{U}}_{a}(x,t)
then the averaged energy integral is

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)]\bigg\rangle=\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}U_{a}(x,t)U^{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)+\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}U_{a}(x,t)U^{a}(x,t){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}U_{a}(x,t)U^{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)+\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}U_{a}(x,t)U^{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}U_{a}(x,t)U^{a}(x,t)d\mu(x)1+\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}(\mathbf{Re}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle={{\mathcal{E}}}[U_{a}(x,t)]1+\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{\mathsf{C}}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}}),t);\mathbb{I})^{2}\equiv\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){{\mathcal{E}}}[U_{a}(x,t)]\leq\bm{\mathcal{Y}}(t){\bm{\mathfrak{B}}}

(7.21)

∎

8.
Vorticity and vortex tangles and correlations

As discussed in the introduction, vorticity and coupled vortex formation over a wide range of length scales, is a salient and universal feature of turbulence
Here, we wish to estimate the vorticity and correlations between vortices with respect to the turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
.

Definition 8.1
.

Given an incompressible fluid velocity
{U}(x,t)\equiv U_{a}(x,t)\bm{e}^{a}
with
\bm{D}.{U}=0
, the vorticity is defined as its curl so that

\displaystyle{\bm{\omega}}(x,t)=curlU(x,t)=D\wedge U(x,t)

(8.1)

or equivalently

\displaystyle{{\bm{\omega}}}_{a}(x,t)={\epsilon}_{ijk}\nabla^{b}U^{k}(x,t)

(8.2)

The vorticity is the measure of the rotation of the fluid locally about a particular point, but not the rotation of the fluid as a whole.

Lemma 8.2
.

The PDE for the vorticity is given by

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}{\bm{\omega}}(x,t)=\nu\Delta{\bm{\omega}}(x,t)+U(x,t).D{\bm{\omega}}(x,t)-{\bm{\omega}}(x,t).DU(x,t)=0

(8.3)

with
D.{\bm{\omega}}(x,t)=0
or

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x,t)-\eta\Delta{\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x)+U_{a}(x)\nabla_{b}{\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x,t)-{\bm{\omega}}^{b}(x,t)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x,t)=0

(8.4)

The time-independent equation is

\displaystyle-\eta\Delta{\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x)+{\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x)\nabla_{b}{\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x)-{\bm{\omega}}_{b}(x)\nabla_{b}U_{a}(x)=0

(8.5)

The term
{\mathbf{\omega}}.D\overrightarrow{U}
is the
vortex stretching term
and is generally difficult to deal with. The vorticity vanishes on a boundary

\partial\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
if the no-slip BC is implemented such that
U_{\partial\bm{\mathfrak{D}}}=0

Lemma 8.3
.

The vorticity and velocity can also be related by a Poisson equation so that

\displaystyle-\Delta U(x)=D\wedge{\bm{\omega}}(x)

(8.6)

which has the solution

\displaystyle U(x)=\frac{1}{4\pi}{\int}_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\frac{\nabla_{(y)}\wedge{\bm{\omega}}(y)d^{3}y}{|x-y|}

(8.7)

or

\displaystyle{U}_{a}(x)=\frac{1}{4\pi}{\int}_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}^{3}}\epsilon_{ijk}\nabla^{b}_{(y)}\left(\frac{1}{|x-y|}\right){\mathrm{V}^{k}(y)d^{3}y}

(8.8)

Integrating by parts gives a Bio-Savart-type law of the form

\displaystyle U_{a}(x)=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\bm{\mathfrak{D}}^{3}}\epsilon_{ijk}\left(\frac{|x^{b}-y^{b}|}{|x-y|^{3}}\right){\mathrm{V}^{k}(y)d^{3}y}

(8.9)

8.1.
Stochastic vorticity

Proposition 8.4
.

Given the turbulent flow
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
as previously defined, the turbulent or stochastic vorticity is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{W}}_{a}(x,t)=\varepsilon_{ijk}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{ijk}\nabla^{b}U^{k}(x,t)+\epsilon_{ijk}\bm{\bm{\alpha}}\nabla^{b}U^{k}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{ijk}{{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)

(8.10)

Taking the stochastic expectation

\displaystyle{\psi}_{a}(x,y;t)={\mathbb{I\!E}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}_{a}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\varepsilon_{ijk}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{ijk}\nabla^{b}U^{k}(x,t)+\epsilon_{ijk}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla^{b}U^{k}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{ijk}{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U^{k}(x,t){{\psi}}(\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}})^{2}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{b}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle={\bm{\omega}}_{a}(x,t)

(8.11)

Proposition 8.5
.

Given
(\mathbf{x,y,z})\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
, the binary and triple vorticity correlations are

\displaystyle{\psi}_{ij}(\mathbf{x,y};t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}_{\ell}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}_{p}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{ilm}\epsilon_{jpq}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{l}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{m}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{p}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{q}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

(8.12)

and

\displaystyle{\psi}_{ijk}(x,y;t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(z,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{ilm}\epsilon_{jpq}\epsilon_{krs}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{l}{\mathscr{U}}^{m}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{p}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{q}(\mathsf{y},t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{r}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{s}(\mathsf{z},t)\bigg\rangle

(8.13)

The N-point correlation is then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}(x_{1},t){{\boxtimes}}...{{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(x_{N},t)\bigg\rangle=\epsilon_{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}}\times...\times\epsilon_{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle(\nabla^{j_{1}}{\mathscr{U}}^{k_{1}}{{\boxtimes}}...{{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{j_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k_{1}}\bigg\rangle

(8.14)

The correlations at a single point
x
then arise in the limit as
(y,z)\rightarrow x
so that

\displaystyle{\psi}_{a}(x,y;t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(x,t)\bigg\rangle=\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\mathscr{\mathscr{W}}(y,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}\epsilon_{ilm}\epsilon_{jpq}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{l}{\mathscr{U}}^{m}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{p}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{q}(y,t)\bigg\rangle=\epsilon_{ilm}\epsilon_{jpq}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{l}{\mathscr{U}}^{m}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{p}{\mathscr{U}}^{q}(x,t)\bigg\rangle

(8.15)

and at 3rd order

\displaystyle\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(z,t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}\epsilon_{ilm}\epsilon_{jpq}\epsilon_{krs}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big[\nabla^{l}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{m}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{p}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{q}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{r}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{s}(z,t)\big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}\epsilon_{ilm}\epsilon_{jpq}\epsilon_{krs}\big\|\!\big\|\nabla^{l}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{m}(x,t)\nabla^{p}D{{\mathscr{U}}}^{q}(y,t){{\mathscr{U}}}^{s}(z,t)\big\|\!\big\|_{E_{1}(\mathfrak{D})}

(8.16)

The N-point correlation is then

\displaystyle\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\mathscr{W}}(x_{1},t){{\boxtimes}}...{{\boxtimes}}{\mathscr{W}}(x_{N},t)\bigg\rangle=\mathcal{E}_{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}}\times...\times\mathcal{E}_{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\nabla^{j_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k_{1}}{{\boxtimes}}...\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}\times\nabla^{j_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k_{1}}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{x,y\rightarrow x}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle\mathcal{E}_{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}}\times...\times\mathcal{E}_{i_{1}j_{1}k_{1}}\bigg|\nabla^{j_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k_{1}}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}...{{\boxtimes}}\nabla^{j_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}^{k_{1}}(x,t)\bigg|\bigg\rangle

(8.17)

The circulation is defined as follows

Definition 8.6
.

(\bm{\mathrm{Definition~of~circulation}})

Let
\Im\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
be an open curve or closed loop in
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
such that
\Im\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
and let
U_{a}(x,t)
be a NS flow within

\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
with
x\in\Im
. The circulation
\mathrm{C}[\Im,t]
is then the line integral

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\Im,t]={\int}_{\Im}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.18)

or for a closed loop or knot
\mathfrak{K}\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\mathfrak{K},t]={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.19)

For an incompressible flow

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\Im,t]={\int}_{\mathcal{S}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}=0,~~~~\nabla_{a}\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\mathfrak{K},t]={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}\nabla_{a}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}=0

(8.20)

The time evolution of the circulations is then

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\Im,t]={\partial}_{t}{\int}_{\Im}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}={\int}_{\Im}{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.21)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\mathfrak{K},t]={\partial}_{t}{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}{\partial}_{t}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.22)

Definition 8.7
.

Kelvin’s circulation theorem for a closed curve or loop
\mathfrak{K}\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
states that for an ideal barytropic fluid with
P=P(\rho)

\displaystyle{{\bm{\mathrm{D}}}}_{m}\bm{\mathrm{C}}[\mathfrak{K},t]=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}+U^{b}\nabla_{b}{\mathrm{C}}[\mathfrak{K},t]=0

(8.23)

Proposition 8.8
.

(\bm{\mathrm{Stochastic~circulation~for~a~turbulent~flow}})

Let
x\in\Im\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
be a loop or knot. As before, let the turbulent fluid flow within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
be the random
field
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
. Then the stochastic circulation is defined as the line integral

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im,t)=\int_{\Im}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Im}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)\int_{\Im}U_{a}(x,t){{\mathscr{B}}}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.24)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Im}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)\int_{\Im}{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{C}}(\Im)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)\int_{\Im}{{\mathscr{R}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.25)

where
{\mathscr{R}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t){\mathscr{B}}(x)
.
For a closed loop

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\mathfrak{K},t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{K}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}{{\mathscr{R}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{C}}(\mathfrak{K},t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

(8.26)

The stochastic average or expectation is then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{C}}}(\mathfrak{K},t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\mathfrak{K},t)\bigg\rangle=\int_{\mathfrak{K}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}\big){\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x)dx^{a}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathrm{C}}(\mathfrak{K},t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}_{a}(x)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}={\mathrm{C}}(\mathfrak{K},t)

(8.27)

since again
{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\big\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\big\rangle=0
.

Equation (8.26) or (8.27) also vanishes as the averaged Reynolds number
\mathrm{R}(\mathscr{W},t)
within
\mathscr{W}
is reduced to or below the critical
Reynolds number.

8.2.
Vortex correlations or ’tangles’ within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}

A set of N knots or curves
\Im_{1},...\Im_{N}\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
can correlated or ’tangled’, which is a key characteristic of turbulence.
(And also of quantum/superfluid turbulence in He3 say.) A correlation of two curves
\Im_{1},\Im_{2}\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
can be denoted
\Im_{1}\bigcap\Im_{2}
; a correlation of three curves or knots is then
\Im_{1}\bigcap\Im_{2}\bigcap\Im_{3}
and so on. For N curves or knots

\displaystyle{{\Im}}_{1}\bigcap{\Im}_{2}\bigcap...\bigcap{\Im}_{N-1}\bigcap{\Im}_{N}

(8.28)

Similarly, for N closed curves or knots
\mathfrak{K}_{1},...\mathfrak{K}_{N}\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
one has

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\bigcap{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\bigcap...\bigcap{\mathfrak{K}}_{N-1}\bigcap{\mathfrak{K}}_{N}

(8.29)

Proposition 8.9
.

Let
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)
and
{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)
be two turbulent flows at
(x,y)\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
at some
t>0
. Then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(x,t){{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle=U_{a}(x,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}\big){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)

(8.30)

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)=U_{a}(y,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}U_{a}(y,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){{\mathscr{B}}}(y)

\displaystyle=U_{a}(y,t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}\big){{\mathscr{B}}}(y)

(8.31)

If
x\in\Im_{1}\subset\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
and
y\in\Im_{2}{\prime}\subset\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
are two curves or knots then the circulations with respect to the underlying NS
flows
U_{a}(x,t)
and
U_{a}(x,t)
are

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{1};t)={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a},~~~~{\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)={\int}_{\Im_{2}}U_{b}(y,t)dy^{b}

(8.32)

The corresponding stochastic circulations are then

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{1};t)

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){{\mathscr{B}}}(x)dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)dx^{a}

\displaystyle\equiv{\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{1};t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)dx^{a}

(8.33)

and

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)dy^{a}={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{b}(y,t)dy^{b}+{\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{b}(y,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}\big){{\mathscr{B}}}(y)dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{b}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(y)dy^{b}

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{2};t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{B}}}(y)dy^{b}

(8.34)

with stochastic expectations

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{1};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(x,t)dx^{a}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{1};t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(x)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}={\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{1};t)

(8.35)

and

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{\int}_{\Im_{1}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)dy^{ij}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{b}(y,t)dy^{b}+{\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{a}(y,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{B}}}(y)\bigg\rangle dy^{a}

\displaystyle={\int}_{\Im_{1}}U_{b}(y,t)dx^{b}+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{R}}}_{b}(y)\bigg\rangle dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{C}}(\Im_{2};t)+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\int}_{\Im_{1}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{R}}}(y)\bigg\rangle dy^{b}={\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)

(8.36)

The correlation of the stochastic vortices is then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}(\Im_{1},\Im_{2};t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{1};t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathbb{I\!E}}\left\langle{\int}\!\!{\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)dx^{a}dy^{b}\right\rangle\equiv{\int}\!\!{\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\int}\!\!{\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)1+{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);\mathbb{I}\big)\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\int}\!\!{\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)dx^{a}dy^{b}

(8.37)

Figure 5
.
Evolution of vortex tangles or correlations within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
with

increasing
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)
.

For two closed knots or loops
(\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}_{2})\in\bm{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{I\!F}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}_{2};t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1};t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{C}}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\left\langle{\oint}\!\!{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)dx^{a}dy^{b}\right\rangle\equiv{\oint}\!\!{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\oint}\!\!{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)1+\bigg|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)\mathsf{C}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\oint}\!\!{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)dx^{a}dy^{b}

\displaystyle+{\oint}\!\!{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)\bigg|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},t){\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\mathfrak{K},t)

\displaystyle+{\oint}\!\!{\oint}_{\mathfrak{K}_{1},\mathfrak{K}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t){\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}^{2}{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big){\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}

(8.38)

An immediate corollary is that the vortices become uncorrelated or ”untangled” for large separations when
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
since the exponential term decays rapidly.

Corollary 8.10
.

when
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda
.

Another corollary is that the vortices are uncorrelated when the volume-averaged Reynold’s number within
\bm{\mathfrak{D}}
falls below the critical value
\bm{\mathrm{R}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
and smooth
or laminar flow is restored.

Corollary 8.11
.

If
\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t)<\bm{\mathrm{Re}}_{c}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}})
for some
t>0
or all
t>0
then the functional
{\psi}
vanishes so that
{{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)=0
.
Then

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}(\Im_{1},\Im_{2};t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{1};t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\int}\!\!\!\!{\int}_{\Im_{1}\Im_{2}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)dx^{a}dy^{b}\equiv{\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{1},t){\bm{\mathrm{C}}}(\Im_{2},t)

(8.39)

The higher-order correlations are similarly defined

Proposition 8.12
.

The 3rd-order correlation for tangle of 3 vortices is

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}(\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3};t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{1};t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{2};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle{\int\!\!\!\!\int\!\!\!\!\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}~{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(z,t)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}dy^{b}dz^{k}

\displaystyle+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{3}{\int\!\!\!\!\int\!\!\!\!\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(z,t){{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|y-z\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}dz^{k}

\displaystyle+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{3}{\int\!\!\!\!\int\!\!\!\!\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(z,t){{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-y\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}dz^{k}

\displaystyle+|{\bm{\bm{\alpha}}}|^{3}{\int\!\!\!\!\int\!\!\!\!\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3}}U_{a}(x,t)U_{b}(y,t)U_{k}(z,t){{\psi}}\big(|\bm{\mathrm{Re}}(\bm{\mathfrak{D}},t);{\mathbb{I}}\big)^{3}{\mathsf{C}}\exp(-\|x-z\|^{2}\lambda^{-2})dx^{a}dy^{b}dz^{k}

(8.40)

The proof simply utilises the results of Section 3 for velocity correlations.

If
\|x-y\|\gg\lambda,\|x-\mathbf{z}\|\gg\lambda,\|y-\mathbf{z}\|\gg\lambda
then this reduces to

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}(\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3};t)={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{1};t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{2};t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{C}}}(\Im_{3};t)\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\left\langle{\int\!\!\!\!\int\!\!\!\!\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(z,t)dx^{a}dy^{b}dz^{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={\int\!\!\!\!\int\!\!\!\!\int}_{\Im_{1},\Im_{2},\Im_{3}}{\bm{\mathbb{I\!E}}}\bigg\langle{{\mathscr{U}}}_{a}(x,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{b}(y,t){{\boxtimes}}{{\mathscr{U}}}_{k}(z,t)\bigg\rangle dx^{a}dy^{b}dz^{k}
